A.2
Robustness to deviations from a CP discriminant tensor

Setup.
We examine CP-TDA’s robustness when the true discriminant tensor
deviates from the ideal CP low-rank structure, using
M=3
with dimensions
(30,30,30)
,
sample sizes
n_{1}=n_{2}=100
, and 1000 test samples. Mode-wise covariances

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=[\Sigma_{m}]_{m=1}^{3}
have unit diagonals with off-diagonal elements

3/d_{m}
. Class means are
{\cal M}_{1}=0
and
{\cal M}_{2}={\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{3}\Sigma_{m}
.
Two misspecification mechanisms of
{\cal B}
are considered:

1. Tucker low-rank with small off-diagonals.
Fix Tucker ranks
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=5
and form the Tucker core as

\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{\text{diag}}+\alpha\mathcal{G}_{\text{off}},\quad\mathcal{G}_{\text{diag}}(i,j,k)=\begin{cases}2&\text{if }i=j=k\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\quad\mathcal{G}_{\text{off}}(i,j,k)\sim\begin{cases}0&\text{if }i=j=k\\
\mathcal{N}(0,1)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{G}_{\text{diag}}\in\mathbb{R}^{5\times 5\times 5}
is a super-diagonal core tensor and
\mathcal{G}_{\text{off}}\in\mathbb{R}^{5\times 5\times 5}
is an off-diagonal perturbation tensor with non-diagonal entries drawn independently from a standard normal distribution. We normalize
\mathcal{G}_{\text{off}}
by scaling it to satisfy
\|\mathcal{G}_{\text{off}}\|_{F}=\|\mathcal{G}_{\text{diag}}\|_{F}
. The off-diagonal energy ratio
\alpha\in\{0.3,0.5,0.7\}
controls the magnitude of deviation from CP low-rank structure. Let
\{\boldsymbol{A}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{30\times 5}\}_{m=1}^{3}
have orthonormal columns. The discriminant tensor is
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{G}\times_{1}\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\times_{2}\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\times_{3}\boldsymbol{A}_{3}
, which reduces to CP low-rank tensor when
\alpha=0
.

2. CP plus sparse perturbation.
Let
\mathcal{B}_{\text{CP}}=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\lambda_{r}\,\boldsymbol{a}_{1r}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{2r}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{3r}
with
R=5,\;\lambda_{r}=2
, and orthonormal bases
\{\boldsymbol{a}_{mr}\}_{m,r}
. Draw a sparse perturbation
\mathcal{U}
by selecting a random subset
\Omega\subset[d_{1}]\times[d_{2}]\times[d_{3}]
of size
K=\lfloor\rho\,d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}\rfloor
, where each index
(i,j,k)
is sampled uniformly without replacement from all possible indices. Then construct

\mathcal{U}(i,j,k)=\begin{cases}\pm\gamma&\text{if }(i,j,k)\in\Omega\\
0&\text{if }(i,j,k)\notin\Omega\end{cases}

where for each
(i,j,k)\in\Omega
, sign is chosen with equal probability, and
\gamma=\tau\|\mathcal{B}_{\text{CP}}\|_{F}/\sqrt{K}
, so that
\|\mathcal{U}\|_{F}=\tau\|\mathcal{B}_{\text{CP}}\|_{F}
. We then set
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\text{CP}}+\mathcal{U}
with
\rho=0.01
and
\tau\in\{0.2,0.4,0.6\}
.

A.3
Non-Gaussian tensor predictors

Setup.
This subsection assesses performance when tensor predictors follow non-Gaussian distributions, specifically tensor elliptical distributions with heavy tails. We consider two families:
(i) The tensor
t
-distribution,
\mathcal{T}t(\mathcal{M},\boldsymbol{\Sigma},\nu)
(Definition 1 in
Wang et al., 2023
), where degrees of freedom
\nu
govern tail heaviness, with smaller
\nu
producing heavier tails.
(ii) The tensor-variate (asymmetric) Laplace distribution,
\mathcal{TL}(\mathcal{M},\boldsymbol{\Sigma},\lambda)
(Definition 2.3 in
Yurchenko, 2021
), where
\lambda
is the rate parameter. Both are implemented via exact samplers taking a mean tensor
\mathcal{M}
and mode-wise covariance matrices
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\{\Sigma_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
; here we use identity covariances.

We adopt the following setting: tensor order
M=3
with dimensions
(30,30,30)
, sample sizes
n_{1}=n_{2}=200
, and the true discriminant tensor
\mathcal{B}
has CP rank
R=5
with orthonormal bases and equal weights
w_{r}=5
. Class means are
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\mathbf{0}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\mathcal{B}
. For the heavy-tail controls, we use
\nu\in\{2,5\}
for the tensor-
t
case and
\lambda\in\{2.0,4.0\}
for the tensor Laplace case. Each configuration is repeated 100 times.

Implementation details.
Methods are evaluated with true ranks/dimensions: GuoSTM uses MPCA output dimensions
(5,5,5)
; LaiSTDA and DATED use mode-wise dimensions
(5,5,5)
; TuckerLDA uses Tucker ranks
(5,5,5)
; ZhouLogitCP is trained with ridge penalties
\rho\in\{0.001,0.005,0.01,\\
0.05,0.1,0.5\}
at rank
R=5
; and CP-TDA uses true rank
R=5
.

t
and tensor-Laplace distributions. Theoretically, based on
Fang and Anderson, 1990
, when tensor predictors follow a tensor elliptical distribution with known class means
\mathcal{M}_{k}
and common covariance matrices
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
, Fisher’s rule remains optimal for Tensor LDA and achieves the minimax misclassification rate. Our simulation results align with this theory: CP-TDA exhibits pronounced robustness to heavy-tailed non-Gaussian settings, consistently achieving the lowest estimation errors and misclassification rates across both distribution families. Moreover, performance improves as distributions become lighter-tailed (larger
\nu
or smaller
\lambda
), with CP-TDA retaining substantial advantages over all competing methods.

A.4
Robustness to heteroscedastic mode-wise covariance matrices

Setup and implementation details.
We study violations of the common mode-wise covariance assumption in CP-TDA using order
M=3
with dimensions
(30,30,30)
, true discriminant tensor
\mathcal{B}
of CP rank
R=5
with orthonormal bases and equal weights
w_{r}=5
. Unlike Section
5
, mode-wise covariance matrices may differ between classes. For class
k\in\{1,2\}
, we set
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(k)}=\{\Sigma_{m}^{(k)}\}_{m=1}^{3}
with
\Sigma_{m}^{(k)}=\sigma_{k}\boldsymbol{I}_{d_{m}}
for
m=1,2,3
, and consider three heteroscedastic configurations
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\{(1,2),(1,2.5),(1,3)\}
. All competing methods use the same ranks and tuning parameters as in Appendix
A.3
. We additionally implement Tensor Gaussianizing Flow (FlowTGMM) proposed in Section
4.1
to handle this heteroscedastic covariance setting. FlowTGMM transforms tensor class-conditional distributions to approximate TGMM with common mode-wise covariance, after which CP-TDA is applied for classification.

. This demonstrates the effectiveness of FlowTGMM in addressing covariance heterogeneity. Overall, CP-TDA remains robust under moderate covariance violations, and combining it with FlowTGMM provides a principled and effective solution for more severe heteroscedasticity.

A.5
Data-driven rank selection and robustness to rank misspecification

Setup and implementation details.
We study performance when the true rank is unknown and must be selected from data. Settings mirror Section
5
: order-3 discriminant tensor of dimensions
(30,30,30)
with true CP rank
R=5
, non-orthogonal CP bases. We consider two signal strength configurations: equal weights
(w_{r}=2)
versus unequal weights
(w_{\max}=4)
.

Rank selection proceeds via 10-fold cross-validation over
R\in\{3,4,5,6,7,10\}
. Methods configure ranks as follows: GuoSTM sets MPCA output dimensions to
(R,R,R)
; LaiSTDA uses mode-wise dimensions
(R,R,R)
; DATED selects latent ranks
(R,R,R)
; TuckerLDA chooses Tucker rank
(R,R,R)
; and CP-TDA uses CP rank
R
. ZhouLogitCP performs model selection via BIC over ranks
R\in\{3,4,5,6,7,10\}
and ridge penalties
\{0.001,0.005,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.5\}
. CATCH requires no rank selection.

Results.
Table
12
presents misclassification rates and most frequently selected ranks across different methods. CP-TDA and TuckerLDA consistently select the correct rank
R=5
across both signal configurations, achieving the lowest misclassification errors. Most competing methods overselect ranks (selecting
R=9
), ZhouLogitCP selects a smaller rank
(R=3)
due to BIC’s penalty for model complexity; both result in substantially higher errors.

R>5
, the inclusion of extra components introduces mild noise, but performance remains close to optimal even for substantially overspecified ranks. The key tradeoff is signal versus noise: underspecification loses discriminant information, while overspecification dilutes signal with noise. Overall, CP-TDA exhibits strong robustness to both misspecification settings.

A.6

Setup and implementation details.

We investigate performance when tensor predictors are ultra high-dimensional. The discriminant tensor
{\cal B}
has CP rank
R=2
with non-orthogonal bases as in Section
5
and equal weights
w_{1}=w_{2}=2
. Tensor dimensions
(256,128,128)
mimic the neuroimaging setup of
Zhou et al., 2013
, so that ambient dimension far exceeds sample size. Each configuration is repeated 100 times. All methods follow the implementation in Appendix
A.1
, adapted to this ultra high-dimensional setting with true rank
R=2
.

Results.
Table
14
summarizes discriminant tensor estimation errors and misclassification rates in the ultra high-dimensional regime. CP-TDA achieves the best performance among all methods; TuckerLDA ranks second with noticeably larger errors, while other competing methods struggle in this extreme setting. Notably, ZhouLogitCP performs poorly despite this setting mimicking their neuroimaging application. This is because their logistic regression framework with ridge penalties, while theoretically motivated, faces substantial computational and statistical challenges in recovering CP low-rank structure when
d\gg n
, as discussed in Appendix
C
.

Appendix C
Limitations of ZhouLogitCP
(
Zhou et al., 2013
)

In this section, we examine the limitations of ZhouLogitCP
(
Zhou et al., 2013
)
and demonstrate the advantages of our CP-TDA for high-dimensional tensor classification.

ZhouLogitCP has three key limitations. First, its optimization is highly non-convex, and the objective function may have exponentially many stationary points in high dimensions
(
Arous et al., 2019
)
. Proposition 1 in
Zhou et al., 2013
assumes the set of stationary points (modulo scaling and permutation indeterminacy) are isolated, similar to Assumption 3 of
Zheng and Wu, 2020
, but this assumption is violated as dimensions increase. Second, the block relaxation algorithm is a variant of Alternating Least Squares (ALS), which requires good initialization for global convergence
(
Kolda and Bader, 2009
;
Tang et al., 2025
)
. ZhouLogitCP uses random initialization, which may converge to local optima arbitrarily far from the global solution, even with multiple restarts. In contrast, our
rc-PCA
initialization provides theoretical guarantees for global convergence. Notably, existing work on high-dimensional logistic regression
(
Han et al., 2022b
)
requires i.i.d. elements of each tensor predictor to construct proper initialization while ours works under correlated elements.
Third, regarding sample efficiency, ZhouLogitCP requires sample size at least as large as the effective number of parameters
p_{e}=R(\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}-M+1)
, otherwise necessitating penalization. CP-TDA only requires
\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}
to be positive definite, imposing the mild condition on the sample size
\min\{n_{1},n_{2}\}>d_{m}/d_{-m}
for each mode
m
.

To isolate and demonstrate the impact of initialization quality, we design an experiment that eliminates the sample efficiency issue. We adopt the following setting: keep the CP-discriminant configuration used earlier but fix the true rank at
R=3
with equal weights
w=2.0
and tensor dimensions
(30,30,30)
. Moreover, we set sample sizes
n_{1}=n_{2}=200
so that we satisfy
Zhou et al., 2013
’s sample requirement, allowing their algorithm to run without penalization.

We compare two initialization mechanisms. First, we use
rc-PCA
initialization with rank
R=3
, yielding
\{\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\text{rcpca}},r\in[3],m\in[3]\}
. We form basis matrices

\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{(0)}=[\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{11}^{\text{rcpca}},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1R}^{\text{rcpca}}],\quad\boldsymbol{A}_{2}^{(0)}=[\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{21}^{\text{rcpca}},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{2R}^{\text{rcpca}}],\quad\boldsymbol{A}_{3}^{(0)}=[\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{31}^{\text{rcpca}},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{3R}^{\text{rcpca}}],

and use
(\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{A}_{2}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{A}_{3}^{(0)})
as a single warm start for ZhouLogitCP’s block relaxation algorithm with fixed rank
R=3
and no ridge penalty. Second, we try varying numbers of random initializations
K\in\{10,50,100,200,500,1000,2000\}
, selecting for each
K
the initialization with largest training log-likelihood
\ell_{\max}^{(K)}
(equivalent to smallest BIC since rank and penalty are fixed). Results from 50 replications are reported in Table
17
.

Comparison of initialization strategies for ZhouLogitCP. ZhouLogitCP w/

;

Table

demonstrates two key findings. First, for ZhouLogitCP,
rc-PCA
initialization substantially outperforms random initialization, confirming the superiority of our principled approach. However, even with
rc-PCA
initialization, ZhouLogitCP still substantially underperforms CP-TDA, likely due to inefficiency of the noisy log-likelihood under moderate sample sizes. Second, while increasing random initializations from
K=10
to
K=2000
monotonically improves training log-likelihood, test misclassification rate remains nearly constant. This indicates that the empirical loss landscape is too noisy and irregular that none of the random initializations land near the global optimum, resulting in poor generalizability even with thousands of restarts.

Appendix D
Training Algorithm for Semiparametric CP-TDA (STDN)

Each training epoch consists of two phases. In the outer snapshot phase, we pass the entire training set through the encoder-flow pipeline to obtain latent tensors
\{{\cal X}_{i}^{(L)}\}_{i=1}^{n_{0}}
under the current parameters
(\beta,\varphi)
and compute the
plug-in statistics
\{\widehat{\pi}_{k},\widehat{\cal M}_{k}^{(L)},\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}^{(L)},\widehat{\cal B}^{cp,(L)}\}
on the full training set. These quantities are frozen for the subsequent inner optimization phase.

In the inner phase, we traverse the training data in mini-batches. For each mini-batch, we
first minimize the empirical cross-entropy loss using logits constructed from

{\cal X}^{(L)}=g_{\varphi}(h_{\beta}({\cal Z}))
and the frozen plug-in statistics, backpropagating gradients
only through
h_{\beta}
to update the encoder parameters
\beta
. We then minimize the mini-batch
FlowTGMM loss
{\cal L}_{n_{0},\mathrm{Flow}}
in Proposition
4.2
, again with

\{\widehat{\cal M}_{k}^{(L)},\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}^{(L)}\}
held fixed, to update the flow parameters
\varphi
.
Algorithm
4
summarizes the complete snapshot-training procedure.

Input :
Dataset
\{({\cal Z}_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n_{0}}
; encoder
h_{\beta}
; flow
g_{\varphi}
; CP rank
R
; batch size
B
; number of epochs
E
; learning rates
\eta_{\beta},\eta_{\varphi}
.

Output :
Trained
(\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\varphi})
and classifier head parameters
(\widehat{\cal B}^{{\rm cp},(L)},\widehat{{\cal M}}_{k}^{(L)},\widehat{\pi}_{1},\widehat{\pi}_{2})
.

1

Initialize
\beta
,
\varphi
.

for

epoch
e=1,\ldots,E

do

2

Outer snapshot phase
:

Compute
{\cal X}_{i}^{(0)}=h_{\beta}({\cal Z}_{i})
and
{\cal X}_{i}^{(L)}=g_{\varphi}({\cal X}^{(0)})
for all
i\in[n_{0}]
.

3

Compute
\{\widehat{\pi}_{k},\widehat{\cal M}_{k}^{(L)},\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}^{(L)},\widehat{\cal B}^{(L)}\}
from
\{{\cal X}_{i}^{(L)},Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{0}}
via equation (
4
).

4

Refine
\widehat{{\cal B}}^{(L)}
using Algorithms
1
-
3
to obtain the CP rank-
R
tensor
\widehat{{\cal B}}^{{\rm cp},(L)}
.

5

Inner optimization phase
:

for

each mini-batch
\{({\cal Z}_{j},Y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{B}

do

6

Compute
{\cal X}_{j}^{(L)}=g_{\varphi}(h_{\beta}({\cal Z}_{j}))
for all
j\in[B]
.

7

Encoder step:
Compute scores
s_{j}\leftarrow\langle\widehat{\cal B}^{{\rm cp},(L)},{\cal X}_{j}^{(L)}-\frac{\widehat{{\cal M}}_{0}^{(L)}+\widehat{{\cal M}}_{1}^{(L)}}{2}\rangle+\log(\widehat{\pi}_{2}/\widehat{\pi}_{1})
.
Update
\beta\leftarrow\beta-\eta_{\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j\in[B]}[Y_{j}\log\sigma(s_{j})+(1-Y_{j})\log(1-\sigma(s_{j}))]
, where
\sigma
is the logistic sigmoid.

8

Flow step:
update
\varphi\leftarrow\varphi-\eta_{\varphi}\nabla_{\varphi}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{B}\Big[-\log f({\cal X}_{j}^{(L)}\mid Y_{j};\widehat{{\cal M}}_{Y_{j}}^{(L)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(L)})-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\log|\det\boldsymbol{J}_{g^{(\ell)}}({\cal X}_{j}^{(\ell-1)})|\Big]
.

return

(\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\varphi},\widehat{\cal B}^{{\rm cp},(L)},\widehat{{\cal M}}_{k}^{(L)},\widehat{\pi}_{1},\widehat{\pi}_{2})

Algorithm 4

Snapshot Training for Semiparametric Tensor Discriminant Network

Appendix E
Proofs for Theorems in Section
3

E.1
Proofs for Initialization (Theorem
3.2
)

Proof I: Noise Decomposition

The initial estimator
\widehat{\cal B}
can be viewed as perturbed version of the ground truth
{\cal B}
where the noise is actually the estimation error. For notational simplicity, we sometimes give explicit expressions only in the case of
m=1
and
M=3
.
We first decompose the perturbing error under
M=3
without loss of generality. The extension towards higher tensor order
M>3
is straightforward.
Let
C_{m,\sigma}=(1-2/n_{L})\mbox{tr}(\otimes_{k\neq m}\Sigma_{k})/d_{-m},\;C_{\sigma}=\prod_{m=1}^{M}C_{m,\sigma}=(1-2/n_{L})^{M}[\mbox{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})/d]^{M-1}=(1-2/n_{L})^{M}[\prod_{m=1}^{M}\mbox{tr}(\Sigma_{m})/d]^{M-1}
.
Recall that from (
2
) and (
4
),

{\cal B}=\llbracket{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1};\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\cal B}=\llbracket\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)};\widehat{\Sigma}_{1}^{-1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket.

Recall

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{\sigma}=\frac{\prod_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{\Sigma}_{m,11}}{\widehat{\Var}({\cal X}_{1\cdots 1})},

where
\widehat{\Var}({\cal X}_{1\cdots 1})
is the pooled sample variance of the first element of
{\cal X}_{i}^{(k)}
.
Define
\boldsymbol{D}_{S}=\mats({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})
. Let
\boldsymbol{U}_{M+m}:=\boldsymbol{U}_{m}
for all
1\leq m\leq M
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{m}=\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}^{-1}-C_{m,\sigma}^{-1}\Sigma_{m}^{-1}:=\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}^{-1}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{m}^{-1}
for
m=1,...,M-1
, and

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{M}=\widehat{\Sigma}_{M}^{-1}-C_{M,\sigma}^{-1}C_{\sigma}\Sigma_{m}^{-1}:=\widehat{\Sigma}_{M}^{-1}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{M}^{-1}
. Due to the identifiability issues associated with the tensor normal distribution, we can rescale the covariance matrices
\Sigma_{m}
such that
C_{m,\sigma}=1
for
1\leq m\leq M-1
. In this sense, we slightly abuse notation by using
\Sigma_{m}
instead of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{m}
.

Then, we have the following decomposition of the error

\displaystyle{\cal E}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\cal B}-{\cal B}=\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket

\displaystyle:={\cal E}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\cal E}_{1,k}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\cal E}_{2,k}+{\cal E}_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\cal E}_{4,k}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\cal E}_{5,k}+{\cal E}_{6},

(26)

where

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{0}

\displaystyle=\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\Sigma_{1}^{-1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{3}

\displaystyle=\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{4,1}

\displaystyle=\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{5,1}

\displaystyle=\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{6}

\displaystyle=\llbracket({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}\rrbracket.

Proof II: Initialization by
c-PCA
without Procedure
2

Taken
\widehat{{\cal B}}
are the perturbed version of the ground truth, we then calculate the initialization through the
rc-PCA
Algorithm
1
.

In this step, we prove a general results of
rc-PCA
on any noisy tensor
\widehat{{\cal B}}={\cal B}+{\cal E}
where
{\cal B}
assumes a CP low-rank structure
{\cal B}=\sum_{r=1}^{R}w_{r}\circ_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}
and
{\cal E}
is the perturbation error tensor, decomposed in (
E.1
).
CP basis for the
m
-th mode
\{\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\}
,
r\in[R]
are not necessarily orthogonal.

We first derive the convergence rate of the CP basis when Procedure
2
is not applied. Specifically, we consider eigengaps satisfy
\min\{w_{i}-w_{i+1},w_{i}-w_{i-1}\}\geq cw_{R}
for all
1\leq i\leq R
, with
w_{0}=\infty,w_{R+1}=0
, and
c
is sufficiently small constant. Let
{\cal S}
be a subset of
[M]
that maximizes
\min(d_{{\cal S}},d_{{\cal S}^{C}})
with
d_{{\cal S}}=\prod_{m\in{\cal S}}d_{m}
and
d_{{\cal S}^{C}}=\prod_{m\in{\cal S}^{C}}d_{m}
.

Let
\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}=\vect(\circ_{m\in S}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
and
\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}=\vect(\circ_{m\in[M]\backslash S}~\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
. Define let
\boldsymbol{A}_{S}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1,S},...,\boldsymbol{a}_{R,S})
and
\boldsymbol{A}_{S^{c}}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1,S^{c}},...,\boldsymbol{a}_{R,S^{c}})
.
Let
\boldsymbol{U}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{u}_{R})
and
\boldsymbol{V}=(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{v}_{R})
be the orthonormal matrices in Lemma
G.1
with
\boldsymbol{A}
and
\boldsymbol{B}
there replaced respectively by
\boldsymbol{A}_{S}
and
\boldsymbol{A}_{S^{c}}
. By Lemma
G.1
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\top}\|_{2}\vee\|\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{v}_{r}\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq\delta,\quad\big\|{\rm mat}_{S}({\cal B})-\boldsymbol{U}\Lambda\boldsymbol{V}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\delta w_{1}.

(27)

Let
{\cal E}^{*}={\rm mat}_{S}({\cal E})={\rm mat}_{S}(\widehat{\cal B}-{\cal B})
. We have

\displaystyle\big\|{\rm mat}_{S}(\widehat{\cal B})-\boldsymbol{U}\Lambda\boldsymbol{V}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\delta w_{1}+\|{\cal E}^{*}\|_{2}.

As
w_{1}>w_{2}>...>w_{R}>w_{R+1}=0
, Wedin’s perturbation theorem
(
Wedin, 1972
)
provides

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\top}\|_{2},\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{v}_{r}\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\top}\|_{2}\right\}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}w_{1}\delta+2\|{\cal E}^{*}\|_{2}}{\min\{w_{r-1}-w_{r},w_{r}-w_{r+1}\}}.

(28)

Combining (
27
) and (
28
), we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}\|_{2},\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S^{c}}^{\top}\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta+\frac{2\sqrt{2}w_{1}\delta+2\|{\cal E}^{*}\|_{2}}{\min\{w_{r-1}-w_{r},w_{r}-w_{r+1}\}}.

(29)

Consider the decomposition of the error term in (
E.1
). The first term
{\cal E}_{0}
is a Gaussian tensor. Specifically, from the assumption on the tensor-variate
{\cal X}
, we have
{\cal E}_{0}\sim{\cal T}{\cal N}(0;\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1})
, where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}=[\Sigma_{m}^{-1}]_{m=1}^{M}
. As
\|\otimes_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}\|_{2}\leq C_{0}
, by Lemma
G.6
, in an event
\Omega_{11}
with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{1}(d_{S}+d_{S^{c}}))
, for all
m=1,2,3
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mats({\cal E}_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d_{S}}+\sqrt{d_{S^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(30)

For
{\cal E}_{1,k},{\cal E}_{2,k},{\cal E}_{3}
, the operator norm of their matricization is of a smaller order compared to that of a Gaussian tensor. By Lemma
G.5
and as
\|\otimes_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq C_{0}
, in an event
\Omega_{12}
with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\exp(-c_{1}d_{m})
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{m}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d_{m}}{nd_{-m}}}.

For the bound of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{3}
, we need to use Lemma
G.5
(ii) with
t_{1}\asymp t_{2}\asymp\log(n)
to derive
|\widehat{C}_{\sigma}-C_{\sigma}|=o(1)
in the event
\Omega_{12}
.
Since
nd_{-m}\gtrsim d_{m}
for all
m
, in the event
\Omega_{12}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{m}\lesssim 1
.
Thus, by Lemma
G.6
, in the event
\Omega_{11}\cap\Omega_{12}
with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{2}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\exp(-c_{2}d_{m})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mats({\cal E}_{1,1})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mats\left(\llbracket(\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)})-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1});\;\boldsymbol{I}_{d_{1}},\Sigma_{2}^{-1},\Sigma_{3}^{-1}\rrbracket\right)\right\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{d_{S}}+\sqrt{d_{S^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{nd_{-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d_{S}}+\sqrt{d_{S^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(31)

for all
m=1,2,3
. Similarly, in the event
\Omega_{11}\cap\Omega_{12}
,
\|\mats({\cal E}_{1,k})\|_{2}
,
\|\mats({\cal E}_{2,k})\|_{2}
and
\|\mats({\cal E}_{3})\|_{2}
,
k=1,2,3
, have the same upper bound in (
31
).
Recall
\boldsymbol{D}_{S}=\mats({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})
, by lemma
G.5
, in the event
\Omega_{12}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mats({\cal E}_{4,k})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}\max_{m}\sqrt{\frac{d_{m}}{nd_{-m}}},\qquad k=1,2,3.

(32)

Again, in the event
\Omega_{12}
,
\|\mats({\cal E}_{5,k})\|_{2}
,
\|\mats({\cal E}_{6})\|_{2}
have the same upper bound in (
32
).
It follows that, in the event
\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{11}\cap\Omega_{12}
with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{2}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\exp(-c_{2}d_{m})
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mats({\cal E})\|_{2}=\|{\cal E}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d_{S}}+\sqrt{d_{S^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}}+C\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}\max_{1\leq m\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{m}}{nd_{-m}}}.

(33)

We formulate each
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{S}}
to be a
|S|
-way tensor
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}
. Let
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{rm}={\rm mat}_{m}(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{r})
, which is viewed as an estimate of
\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\vect(\circ_{\ell\in S\backslash\{m\}}~\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}\times(d_{S}/d_{m})}
. Then
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\rm rcpca}
is the top left singular vector of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{rm}
.
By Lemma
G.2
, for any
m\in S

\displaystyle\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\rm rcpca}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{{\rm rcpca}\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}\wedge(1/2)\leq\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}.

Similar bound can be obtained for
\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\rm rcpca}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{{\rm rcpca}\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{2}

for
m\in S^{c}
. Substituting (
29
) and (
33
) into the above equation, as
\|\mats({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})\|_{2}\asymp w_{1}
, we have the desired results.

Proof III: Initialization by
rc-PCA

Consider the situation when Procedure
2
is applied.

Step I.
Random projections in Procedure
2
create desirable eigen-gaps between the first and second largest eigen-values, and achieve desired upper bounds.

Let
\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}}=\vect(\circ_{m\in S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
and
\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}^{c}}=\vect(\circ_{m\in S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
,
\Lambda=\diag(w_{1},...,w_{R})
and
w_{1}\geq w_{2}\geq\cdots\geq w_{R}
. Define
\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1,S_{1}},...,\boldsymbol{a}_{R,S_{1}})
and
\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}^{c}}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1,S_{1}^{c}},...,\boldsymbol{a}_{R,S_{1}^{c}})
. Let
\delta_{k}=\|\boldsymbol{A}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{k}-I_{R}\|_{2}
and
\delta_{S_{1}}=\|\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}}-I_{R}\|_{2}
. Note that
\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}=\|\mats({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})\|_{2}\asymp w_{1}
.

Lemma E.1
.

Assume
\delta_{1}(w_{1}/w_{R})\leq c
for a sufficiently small positive constant
c
.
Apply random projection in Procedure
2
to the whole sample discriminant tensor
\widehat{\cal B}
with
L\geq Cd_{1}^{2}\vee Cd_{1}R^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}}
. Denote the estimated CP basis vectors as
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}
, for
1\leq\ell\leq L,1\leq m\leq M
. Then in an event with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-d_{1}^{-c}-\sum_{m=2}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
, we have for any CP basis vectors tuple
(\boldsymbol{a}_{rm},1\leq m\leq M)
, there exist
j_{r}\in[L]
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{j_{r},m}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{j_{r},m}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\psi_{i},\qquad 2\leq m\leq M,

(34)

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{j_{r},1}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{j_{r},1}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\psi_{i}+C\sqrt{R-1}\prod_{m=2}^{M}\delta_{m}(w_{1}/w_{R})+C\sqrt{R-1}\psi_{i}^{M-1}(w_{1}/w_{R}),

(35)

where
1\leq r\leq R
and

\displaystyle\psi_{i}=C\left(\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}}}+\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{i}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}}{w_{i}}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}}\right)+\frac{2\delta_{1}w_{1}}{w_{R}}.

(36)

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle\Xi(\theta)={\rm mat}_{S_{1}}(\widehat{\cal B}\times_{1}\theta)=\sum_{r=1}^{R}w_{r}(\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\theta)\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}+{\rm mat}_{S_{1}}({\cal E}\times_{1}\theta).

First, consider the upper bound of
\|{\rm mat}_{S_{1}}({\cal E}\times_{1}\theta)\|_{2}
. By concentration inequality for matrix Gaussian sequence (see, for example Theorem 4.1.1 in
Tropp et al., 2015
) and employing similar arguments in (
33
) of Proof II, we have, in an event
\Omega_{0}
with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-d_{1}^{-c}-\sum_{m=2}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
,

\displaystyle\|{\rm mat}_{S_{1}}({\cal E}\times_{1}\theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{d_{1}}\theta_{i}{\cal E}_{i\cdot\cdot}\right\|_{2}\leq C\max\left\{\left\|{\rm mat}_{(12),(3)}({\cal E})\right\|_{2},\left\|{\rm mat}_{(13),(2)}({\cal E})\right\|_{2}\right\}\cdot\sqrt{\log(d_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}}}+\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}\max_{1\leq m\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{m}}{nd_{-m}}}\right)\sqrt{\log(d_{1})}.

(37)

When
M=3
,
{\cal E}_{i\cdot\cdot}
represents the
i
-th slice of
{\cal E}
,
{\rm mat}_{(12),(3)}(\cdot)
denotes the reshaping of order-three tensor into a matrix by collapsing its first and second indices as rows, and the third indices as columns. In the last step, we apply the arguments in (
33
) of Proof II.

Consider the
i
-th factor and rewrite
\Xi(\theta)
as follows

\displaystyle\Xi(\theta)=w_{i}(\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta)\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}+\sum_{r\neq i}^{R}w_{r}(\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\theta)\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}+{\rm mat}_{S_{1}}({\cal E}\times_{1}\theta).

(38)

Suppose now we repeatedly sample
\theta_{\ell}\sim\theta
, for
\ell=1,...,L
. By the anti-concentration inequality for Gaussian random variables (see Lemma B.1 in
Anandkumar et al., 2014a
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}\leq\sqrt{2\log(L)}-\frac{\log\log(L)}{4\sqrt{\log(L)}}-\sqrt{2\log(8)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{4},

(39)

where
\odot
denotes Kronecker product. Let

\displaystyle\ell_{*}=\arg\max_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}.

Note that
\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}
and
(I_{d_{1}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top})\theta_{\ell}
are independent. Since the definition of
\ell_{*}
depends only on
\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}
, this implies that the distribution of
(I_{d_{1}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top})\theta_{\ell}
does not depend on
\ell_{*}
.

By Gaussian concentration inequality of
1
-Lipschitz function, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{r\leq R}\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\big(I_{d_{1}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\big)\theta_{\ell}\geq\sqrt{4\log(R)}+\sqrt{2\log(8)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Moreover, for the reminder bias term
\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}
, we have,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{r\neq i}w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}}\left(\Lambda\odot\left(\diag(\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1})-e_{ii}\right)\right)\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}\|\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{A}_{S_{1}^{c}}\|_{2}\|\Lambda\|_{2}\left\|\diag(\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1})-e_{ii}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta_{S_{1}}\vee\delta_{S_{1}^{c}})\delta_{1}w_{1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell},

where
\odot
denotes Hadamard product,
e_{ii}
is a
d_{1}\times d_{1}
matrix with the
(i,i)
-th element be 1 and all the others be 0.

Thus, we obtain the top eigengap

\displaystyle w_{i}(\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta)-\left\|\sum_{r\neq i}^{R}w_{r}(\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}^{\top}\theta)\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-2\delta_{1}w_{1}/w_{i})\left(\sqrt{2\log(L)}-\frac{\log\log(L)}{4\sqrt{\log(L)}}-\sqrt{2\log(8)}\right)w_{i}-\left(\sqrt{4\log(R)}+\sqrt{2\log(8)}\right)w_{i}(w_{1}/w_{i})

\displaystyle\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log(d_{1})}w_{i},

(40)

with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
, by letting
L\geq Cd_{1}\vee CR^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}}
.

Since
\theta_{\ell}
are independent samples, we instead take
L_{i}=L_{i1}+\cdots+L_{iK}
for
K=\lceil C_{1}\log(d_{1})/\log(2)\rceil
and
L_{i1},...,L_{iK}\geq Cd_{1}\vee CR^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}}
. We define

\displaystyle\ell_{*}^{(k)}=\arg\max_{1\leq\ell\leq L_{ik}}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell},\quad\ell_{*}=\arg\max_{1\leq\ell\leq L_{i}}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\theta_{\ell}.

We then have, by independence of
\theta_{\ell}
, that the above statement (
E.1
) for the
i
-th factor holds in an event
\Omega_{i}
with probability at least
1-d_{1}^{-C_{1}}
. By Wedin’s perturbation theory, we have in the event
\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell_{*}}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell_{*}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}}\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\vee\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell_{*}}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell_{*}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}^{c}}\boldsymbol{a}_{i,S_{1}^{c}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|{\rm mat}_{S_{1}}({\cal E}\times_{1}\theta)\|_{2}}{w_{i}\sqrt{\log(d_{1})}}+\frac{2\delta_{1}w_{1}}{w_{R}},

where
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell_{*}}
and
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell_{*}}
are the top left and right singular vector of
\Xi(\theta_{\ell_{*}})
. By Lemma
G.2
and (
E.1
),

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}}}+\sqrt{d_{1}d_{S_{1}^{c}}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{i}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{S}\|_{2}}{w_{i}}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}}\right)+\frac{2\delta_{1}w_{1}}{w_{R}},

(41)

for all
2\leq m\leq M,1\leq i\leq R.

Now consider to obtain
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},1}
. Write
\psi_{i}
to be the error bound on the right hand side of (
41
). Note that

\displaystyle\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=2}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}=

\displaystyle\prod_{m=2}^{M}\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\right)w_{i}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}+\sum_{r\neq i}\prod_{m=2}^{M}\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\right)w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}+{\cal E}\times_{m=2}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}.

By (
41
), in the event
\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal E}\times_{m=2}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}\right\|_{2}\leq\|{\cal E}\|_{\rm op},

\displaystyle\prod_{m=2}^{M}\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\right)\geq(1-\psi_{i}^{2})^{(M-1)/2}.

Since

\displaystyle\max_{j\neq i}\big|\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}\big|

\displaystyle=\max_{j\neq i}\big|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}+\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}(I-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top})\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\neq i}\big|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\big|\big|\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}\big|+\max_{j\neq i}\big\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}(I-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top})\big\|_{2}\big\|(I-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top})\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-\psi_{i}^{2}}\delta_{m}+\psi_{i}\sqrt{1-\delta_{m}^{2}}\leq\delta_{m}+\psi_{i},

(42)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{r\neq i}\prod_{m=2}^{M}\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\right)w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(R-1)(1+\delta_{1})\prod_{m=2}^{M}(\delta_{m}+\psi_{i})^{2}w_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}(R-1)\left(\prod\nolimits_{m=2}^{M}\delta_{m}^{2}+\psi_{i}^{2M-2}\right)w_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}\left(\prod\nolimits_{m=3}^{M}\delta_{m}^{2}+\psi_{i}^{2M-4}\right)w_{1}^{2}.

By Wedin’s perturbation theory,

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},1}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},1}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i1}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\leq\psi_{i}+C\sqrt{R-1}\prod_{m=2}^{M}\delta_{m}(w_{1}/w_{R})+C\sqrt{R-1}\psi_{i}^{M-1}(w_{1}/w_{R}).

(43)

Repeat the same argument again for all
1\leq i\leq R
factors, and let
L=\sum_{i}L_{i}\geq Cd_{1}^{2}\vee Cd_{1}R^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}}\geq Cd_{1}R\log(d_{1})\vee CR^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}+1}\log(d_{1})
. We have, in the event
\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\Omega_{R}
with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-d_{1}^{-c}-\sum_{m=2}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
, (
41
) and (
43
) hold for all
i
.

∎

Step II.
Clustering.

For simplicity, consider the most extreme case where
\min\{w_{i}-w_{i+1},w_{i}-w_{i-1}\}\leq cw_{R}
for all
i
, with
w_{0}=\infty,w_{R+1}=0
, and
c
is sufficiently small constant. In such cases, we need to employ Procedure
2
to the entire sample discriminant tensor
\widehat{\cal B}
. Let the eigenvalue ratio
w_{1}/w_{R}=O(R)
. In general, the statement in the theorem holds for number of initialization
L\geq Cd_{1}^{2}\vee Cd_{1}R^{2(w_{1}/w_{R})^{2}}
, where
a\vee b=\max\{a,b\}
.
We prove the statements through induction on factor index
i
starting from
i=1
proceeding to
i=R
. By the induction hypothesis, we already have estimators such that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\rm rcpca}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\rm rcpca\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0},\qquad 1\leq j\leq i-1,1\leq m\leq M,

(44)

in an event
\Omega
with high probability, where

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{2}=C\left(\psi_{R}+\sqrt{R-1}\left(\psi_{R}\right)^{M-1}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{R}}\right)+\max_{2\leq m\leq M}\delta_{m}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{R}}\right)\right).

Recall
\psi_{i}
is defined in (
36
).

Applying Lemma
E.1
, we obtain that at the
i
-th step (
i
-th factor), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{0}^{2},\qquad 1\leq m\leq M,

in the event
\Omega
with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-d_{1}^{-c}-\sum_{m=2}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
for at least one
\ell\in[L]
. It follows that this estimator
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{R}w_{j}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}-\left\|{\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|w_{i}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\sum_{j\neq i}^{R}w_{j}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}-\left\|{\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}.

By (
E.1
) and the last part of the proof of Lemma
E.1
, as
\left\|{\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}/w_{i}\leq\phi_{0}^{2}
and
C\sqrt{R-1}(1+\delta_{1})\prod_{m=2}^{M}(\delta_{m}+\psi_{i})(w_{1}/w_{R})\leq\phi_{0}^{2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\phi_{0}^{4})^{\frac{M}{2}}w_{i}-C\sqrt{R-1}(1+\delta_{1})\prod_{m=2}^{M}(\delta_{m}+\psi_{i})w_{1}-\phi_{0}^{2}w_{i}

\displaystyle\geq(1-3\phi_{0}^{2})w_{i}.

Now consider the best initialization
\ell_{*}\in[L]
by using
\ell_{*}=\arg\max_{s}|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{sm}|
. By the calculation above, it is immediate that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-3\phi_{0}^{2})w_{i}.

(45)

If
\|\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}^{\top}-\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*}}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*}}^{\top}\|_{2}\geq C\phi_{0}
for a sufficiently large constant
C
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{R}w_{j}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}+\left\|{\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{j=i}^{R}w_{j}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm}^{\top}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}+\phi_{0}^{2}w_{i}+R\nu^{M}w_{1}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\max_{m}\delta_{m})(1-C^{2}\phi_{0}^{2}/2)w_{i}+\phi_{0}^{2}w_{i}+R\nu^{M}w_{1}.

If
\nu
satisfies
R\nu^{M}(w_{1}/w_{R})\leq c\phi_{0}^{2}
for a small positive constant
c
, as
\max_{m}\delta_{m}\leq\phi_{0}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell m}\right\|_{2}\leq(1-C^{\prime}\phi_{0}^{2})w_{i},

where
C^{\prime}
is a sufficiently large constant. It contradicts (
45
) above. This implies that for
\ell=\ell_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}^{\top}-\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*}}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*}}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq C\phi_{0}.

By Lemma
G.2
, with
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\rm rcpca}=\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell_{*},m}
, in the event
\Omega
with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-d_{1}^{-c}-\sum_{m=2}^{M}e^{-cd_{M}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\rm rcpca}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\rm rcpca\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{im}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0},\quad 1\leq m\leq M.

This finishes the proof by an induction argument along with the requirements
R\nu^{M}(w_{1}/w_{R})\leq c\phi_{0}^{2}
. The general upper bound of
rc-PCA
is provided in (
44
).

E.2
Proof of Theorem
3.1
for Algorithm
3
DISTIP-CP

Step I.

Upper bound for
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}
.

Recall that
\boldsymbol{B}_{m}=\boldsymbol{A}_{m}(\boldsymbol{A}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{m})^{-1}=(\boldsymbol{b}_{1m},\cdots,\boldsymbol{b}_{Rm})
with
\boldsymbol{A}_{m}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1m},\cdots,\boldsymbol{a}_{Rm})
. Let
\boldsymbol{g}_{rm}=\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}/\|\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}\|_{2}
,
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{rm}^{(t)}=\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{(t)}/\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{(t)}\|_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\sqrt{(1-\delta_{\max})(1-1/(4R))}-(R^{1/2}+1)\psi_{0}.

Let
\psi_{0,\ell}=\psi_{0}
and define sequentially

\displaystyle\phi_{t,m-1}

\displaystyle=(M-1)\alpha^{-1}\sqrt{2R/(1-1/(4R))}\max_{1\leq\ell<M}\psi_{t,m-\ell},

\displaystyle\psi_{t,m}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\alpha^{1-M}\sqrt{R-1}(w_{1}/w_{R})\prod\nolimits_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\psi_{t,m-\ell}\Big)\vee\Big(C\alpha^{1-M}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{Rm,\phi_{t,m-1}}\Big),

(46)

\displaystyle\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{rm,\phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{d_{m}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{r}}+(\phi\wedge 1)\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sqrt{d_{k}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{r}}+\frac{\|{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1}\|_{\rm op}}{w_{r}}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}},

for
m=1,\ldots,M
,
t=1,2,\ldots

By induction, (
9
) and
\alpha>0,\rho<1
give
\psi_{t,m}\leq\psi_{t-1,m}\leq\psi_{0}
.
Here and in the sequel, we take the convention that
(t,\ell)=(t-1,M+\ell)
with the subscript
(t,\ell)
, and that
\times_{\ell}\widehat{\theta}_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=\times_{M+\ell}\widehat{\theta}_{j,M+\ell}^{(t-1)}
for any estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{j\ell}^{(t)}
.
Let

\displaystyle\Omega^{*}_{t,m-1}=\cap_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\Omega_{t,m-\ell}

with
\Omega_{t,\ell}=\big\{\max\nolimits_{r\leq R}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{(t)}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{(t)\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq\psi_{t,\ell}\big\}
.
Given
\{\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)},r\in[R],\ell\in[M-1]\}
, the
t
-th iteration for tensor mode
m
produces estimates
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}
as the normalized version of
\widehat{\cal B}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}
. Because

\widehat{\cal B}=\sum_{r=1}^{R}w_{r}\circ_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}+{\cal E}
, the “noiseless” version of this update is given by

\widehat{\cal B}\times_{l=1,l\neq m}^{M}\boldsymbol{b}_{rl}=w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}+{\cal E}\times_{l=1,l\neq m}^{M}\boldsymbol{b}_{rl}^{\top}.

(47)

Similarly, for any
1\leq r\leq R
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\cal B}_{rm}^{(t)}:=\widehat{\cal B}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\widetilde{w}_{i,r}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}+{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}},

where

\widetilde{w}_{i,r}=w_{i}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\boldsymbol{a}_{i,m-\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}
.
At
t
-th iteration,
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}=\widetilde{\cal B}_{rm}^{(t)}/\|\widetilde{\cal B}_{rm}^{(t)}\|_{2}
.

We may assume without loss of generality
\boldsymbol{a}_{j\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{j\ell}^{(t)}\geq 0
for all
(j,\ell)
.
Similar to the proofs of Proposition 4 and Theorem 3 in
Han and Zhang, 2023
, we can show

\displaystyle\max_{r\leq R}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}\|_{2}\leq\psi_{t,\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)},\ \ \displaystyle\big\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r\ell}^{(t)}\big\|_{2}\leq\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}_{\ell}^{(t)}\|_{\rm 2}\leq\bigg(\sqrt{1-\delta_{\ell}}-\frac{R^{1/2}\psi_{0}}{\sqrt{1-1/(4R)}}\bigg)^{-1},

(48)

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{b}_{r\ell}/\|\boldsymbol{b}_{r\ell}\|_{2}\big\|_{2}\leq(\psi_{t,\ell}/\alpha)\sqrt{2R/(1-1/(4R))}.

(49)

Moreover,
(
48
) provides

\displaystyle\max_{i\neq r}\big|\boldsymbol{a}_{i\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{(t)}\big|\leq\psi_{t,\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)},\ \ \big|\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{(t)}\big|\geq\alpha,

(50)

as
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{i\ell}^{(t)\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{(t)}=I\{i=r\}/\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r\ell}^{(t)}\|_{2}
.
Then, for
i\neq r
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i,r}/\widetilde{w}_{r,r}

\displaystyle=\big(w_{1}/w_{r}\big)\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\frac{\left|(\boldsymbol{a}_{i,m-\ell}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{i,m-\ell}^{(t)})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}\right|}{\left|1+(\boldsymbol{a}_{r,m-\ell}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\big(w_{1}/w_{r}\big)\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\frac{[\psi_{t,m-\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)}]/[\sqrt{1-\delta_{\ell}}-R^{1/2}\psi_{t,m-\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)}]}{1-[\psi_{t,m-\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)}]/[\sqrt{1-\delta_{\ell}}-R^{1/2}\psi_{t,m-\ell}/\sqrt{1-1/(4R)}]}

\displaystyle\leq\big(w_{1}/w_{r}\big)\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\frac{\psi_{t,m-\ell}}{\alpha}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{R}\widetilde{w}_{i,r}\boldsymbol{a}_{im}/\widetilde{w}_{r,r}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq r}^{R}\sum_{j\neq r}^{R}(\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{jm})(\widetilde{w}_{i,r}/\widetilde{w}_{r,r})(\widetilde{w}_{j,r}/\widetilde{w}_{r,r})

(51)

\displaystyle\leq(R-1)(1+\delta_{m})\big(w_{1}/w_{r}\big)^{2}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\left(\frac{\psi_{t,m-\ell}}{\alpha}\right)^{2}.

(52)

By basic geometry, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{\rm 2}

\displaystyle=\|\sin\angle(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})\|_{2}\leq\|\widetilde{\cal B}_{rm}^{(t)}/\widetilde{w}_{r,r}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{w_{1}\sqrt{(R-1)(1+\delta_{m})}}{w_{r}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\frac{\psi_{t,m-\ell}}{\alpha}+\frac{\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\|_{2}}{w_{r}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,m-\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}}.

(53)

in
\Omega^{*}_{t,m-1}
. As
{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}
is linear in each
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{(t)}
,

\displaystyle\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\big\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle(M-1)\max_{\ell<M}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{g}_{r,m-\ell}\|_{2}\|\Delta\|+\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell\in[M]\backslash\{m\}}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{\top}\big\|_{2},

where
\|\Delta\|=\max_{v_{\ell}\in\mathbb{S}^{d_{\ell}-1}\forall\ell}\big({\cal E}\times_{\ell=1}^{M}v_{\ell}^{\top}\big)
.
As we also have
\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\|\Delta\|
,
(
49
) and (
50
) yield

\displaystyle\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\big\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\min\big\{\|\Delta\|,\phi_{t,m-1}\|\Delta\|+\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell\in[M]\backslash\{m\}}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{\top}\big\|_{2}

(54)

in
\Omega^{*}_{t,m-1}
, in view of the definition of
\phi_{t,m-1}
in (
46
).
By the Sudakov-Fernique and Gaussian concentration inequalities, similar to the proof of (
33
), we can show

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|\leq C\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sqrt{d_{k}}}{\sqrt{n}}+C\|{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1}\|_{\rm op}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}},

\displaystyle\big\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell\in[M]\backslash\{m\}}\boldsymbol{g}_{r\ell}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d_{m}}}{\sqrt{n}}+C\|{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1}\|_{\rm op}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}},

in an event
\Omega_{1}
with at least probability
1-n^{-c}-\sum_{k=1}^{M}e^{-cd_{k}}
.
Consequently, by (
54
), in
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega^{*}_{m,k-1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\cal E}\times_{\ell=m-1}^{m-M+1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,\ell}^{(t)\top}\|_{2}}{w_{r}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\boldsymbol{a}_{r,m-\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{g}_{r,m-\ell}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\alpha^{1-M}\sqrt{d_{m}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{r}}+\frac{C\alpha^{1-M}\|{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1}\|_{\rm op}}{w_{r}}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}}{nd_{-k}}}+C\alpha^{1-M}(\phi_{t,m-1}\vee 1)\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sqrt{d_{k}}}{\sqrt{n}w_{r}}.

(55)

Substituting (
E.2
) into (
54
), we have, in the event
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{t,m-1}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{\rm 2}\leq\psi_{t,r,m}

(56)

with

\displaystyle\psi_{t,r,m}=\max\bigg\{C\alpha^{1-M}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{rm,\phi_{t,m-1}},\frac{w_{1}\sqrt{2R-2}}{w_{r}\alpha^{M-1}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\psi_{t,m-\ell}\bigg\}.

Consequently,
\Omega_{t,m}\subset\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega^{*}_{t,m-1}
.
Let
\Omega_{0}=\{\max_{r\leq R,m\leq M}\;\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(0)}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(0)\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq\psi_{0}\}

for any initial estimates
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(0)}
. Then (
56
) holds in the event
\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{1}
.

Step II.

Number of iterations.

We now consider the number of iterations and the convergence of
\psi_{t,m}
in (
46
). A simple way of dealing with the dynamics of (
46
) is to compare
\psi_{t,r,m}
with

\displaystyle\psi^{*}_{t,r,m}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\alpha^{1-M}\sqrt{R-1}(w_{1}/w_{r})\prod\nolimits_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\psi^{*}_{t,m-\ell}\Big)\vee\Big(C\alpha^{1-M}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{rm,1}\Big)

\displaystyle\psi^{*}_{t,m}

\displaystyle=\psi^{*}_{t,R,m},

(57)

with initialization
\psi^{*}_{0,r,m}=\psi_{0}
. Compared with (
46
), (
E.2
) is easier to analyze due to
the use of static 1 in
\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{rm,\phi_{t,m-1}}
and the monotonicity of
\psi^{*}_{t,m}
in
m
. It follows that

\displaystyle\psi_{t,r,m}\leq\psi^{*}_{t,r,m}\leq\psi^{*}_{t,m},\quad\forall(t,r,m).

(58)

As
\rho<1
with
\rho
defined in (
9
),
\psi_{1,1}^{*}\leq\rho\psi_{0}\vee\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{R1,1}
and this would contribute the extra factor
\rho
in the application (
E.2
) to
\psi_{1,2}^{*}
, resulting in
\psi_{1,2}^{*}\leq\rho^{2}\psi_{0}\vee\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{R1,1}
, so on and so forth. In general,
\psi_{t,m}^{*}\leq(\rho^{T_{(t-1)M+m}}\psi_{0})\vee\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{R1,1}
with
T_{1}=1
,
T_{2}=2,\ldots,T_{M}=2^{M-1}
, and
T_{k+1}=1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{M-1}T_{k+1-\ell}
for
k>M
. By induction, for
k=M,M+1,\ldots
.

\displaystyle T_{k+1}\geq\gamma_{M}^{k-1}+\cdots+\gamma_{M}^{k-M+1}=\gamma_{M}^{k}\frac{1-\gamma_{M}^{-M+1}}{\gamma_{M}-1}=\gamma_{M}^{k}.

The function
f(\gamma)=\gamma^{M}-2\gamma^{M-1}+1
is decreasing in
(1,2-2/M)
and increasing
(2-2/M,\infty)
.
Because
f(1)=0
and
f(2)=1>0
, we have
2-2/M<\gamma_{M}<2
. It follows that the required number of iteration is at most
T=\lceil M^{-1}\{1+(\log\gamma_{M})^{-1}\log\log(\psi_{0}/\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{R1,1})/\log(1/\rho)\}\rceil
. Furthermore, the desired upper bound for
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}
after convergence is

\displaystyle\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{(t)\top}-\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\|_{\rm 2}\leq C\alpha^{1-M}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}

(59)

in the event
\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{1}
.

Step III.

Upper bound for
\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}
.

After convergence, let
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}=\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{(t)}
.
For weights estimation, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{w}_{r}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{\top}={\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{\top}+{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{\top}

\displaystyle={\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{\top}+w_{r}\prod_{m=1}^{M}(\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm})+\sum_{i\neq r}w_{i}\prod_{m=1}^{M}(\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}).

As
\sqrt{R}\psi_{t,m}<1
and
\rho<1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{w}_{r}-w_{r}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|{\cal E}\times_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm}^{\top}\right|+w_{r}\left|\prod_{m=1}^{M}(\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm})-1\right|+\left|\sum_{i\neq r}w_{i}\prod_{m=1}^{M}(\boldsymbol{a}_{im}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{b}_{rm})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{-M}w_{r}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}+w_{r}\left(1-\prod_{m=1}^{M}(1-\alpha^{-1}\psi_{t,m})\right)+\left|\sum_{i\neq r}w_{i}\alpha^{-M}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\psi_{t,m}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{-M}w_{r}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}+\alpha^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{M}w_{r}\psi_{t,r,m}+\alpha^{-M}w_{R}(R-1)(w_{1}/w_{R})\prod_{m=1}^{M}\psi_{t,m}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{-M}w_{r}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}+\alpha^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{M}w_{r}\psi_{t,r,m}+\alpha^{-M}w_{R}\min_{m}\psi_{t,m}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}w_{r}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1},

which is free of
w_{r}
by the definition of
\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}
.

We may assume without loss of generality
\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r\ell}\geq 0
for all
(r,\ell)
.
Let
\boldsymbol{a}_{r}=\vect(\circ_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
and
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}=\vect(\circ_{m=1}^{M}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm})
.
Employing similar arguments in the proof of (
51
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{{\cal B}}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sum_{r\in[R]}\widehat{w}_{r}\circ_{m\in[M]}\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{rm}-\sum_{r\in[R]}w_{r}\circ_{m\in[M]}\boldsymbol{a}_{rm}\right\rVert_{\rm F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sum_{r\in[R]}\widehat{w}_{r}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}-\sum_{r\in[R]}w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}\right\rVert_{\rm 2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\sum_{r\in[R]}(\widehat{w}_{r}-w_{r})\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\sum_{r\in[R]}w_{r}\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}-\sum_{r\in[R]}w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\sum_{r\in[R]}(\widehat{w}_{r}-w_{r})\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}\right\rVert_{2}+\sqrt{R}\max_{r\leq R}\left\lVert w_{r}\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}-w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{R}\max_{r\leq R}\left\lvert\widehat{w}_{r}-w_{r}\right\rvert+\sqrt{R}\max_{r\leq R}\left\lVert w_{r}\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}-w_{r}\boldsymbol{a}_{r}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\sqrt{R}w_{r}\eta^{(\text{\footnotesize ideal})}_{r1,1}.

Note that
\|{\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1}\|_{\rm op}\asymp w_{1}
. We can further simplify the bounds.

E.3
Proof of Theorem
3.3

For simplicity, we mainly focus on the proof for a simple scenario where the prior probabilities
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=1/2
. Additionally, we will provide key steps of the proof for more general settings correspondingly. Let
\widehat{\Delta}=\sqrt{\langle\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}
, the misclassification error of
\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp}
is

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{L_{1}}}{n_{L_{1}}+n_{L_{2}}}\phi\left(\widehat{\Delta}^{-1}\log(n_{L_{2}}/n_{L_{1}})-\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{n_{L_{2}}}{n_{L_{1}}+n_{L_{2}}}\overline{\phi}\left(\widehat{\Delta}^{-1}\log(n_{L_{2}}/n_{L_{1}})-\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right)

and the optimal misclassification error is

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\rm opt}=\pi_{1}\phi(\Delta^{-1}\log(\pi_{2}/\pi_{1})-\Delta/2)+\pi_{2}\overline{\phi}(\Delta^{-1}\log(\pi_{2}/\pi_{1})+\Delta/2),

where
\phi
is the CDF of the standard normal, and
\overline{\phi}(\cdot)=1-\phi(\cdot)
.
While the simpler version when
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=\frac{1}{2},
are

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})=\frac{1}{2}\phi\left(-\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\phi}\left(-\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right)

and
{\cal R}_{\rm opt}=\phi(-\Delta/2)=\frac{1}{2}\phi(-\Delta/2)+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\phi}(\Delta/2),
respectively.
Define an intermediate quantity

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{*}=\frac{1}{2}\phi\left(-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\phi}\left(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\right).

By Theorem
3.1
, in an event
\Omega_{1}
with probability at least
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})-n^{-c_{1}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}e^{-c_{1}d_{m}}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}d_{k}R}}{\sqrt{n}}+Cw_{1}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}R}{nd_{-k}}}=o(\Delta).

(60)

Firstly, we are going to show that
R^{*}-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\lesssim e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\cdot\Delta^{-1}\cdot\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
.
Applying Taylor’s expansion to the two terms in
{\cal R}^{*}
at
-\Delta/2
and
\Delta/2
, respectively, we obtain

\begin{split}{\cal R}^{*}-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=&\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\Delta}{2}-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\frac{\Delta}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\Delta}{2}-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(-\frac{\Delta}{2})\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\right)^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{1,n})+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\right)^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{2,n})\end{split}

(61)

where
t_{1,n},\;t_{2,n}
are some constants satisfying
|t_{1,n}|,\;|t_{2,n}|
are between
\frac{\Delta}{2}
and
\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}
.

Since
\big(\frac{\Delta}{2}-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\big)
frequently appears in (
61
), we need to bound its absolute value. Let
\gamma={\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{1/2}
and
\widehat{\gamma}=\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{1/2}
,
then by Lemma
G.9
, in the event
\Omega_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\Delta-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right|=\left|\|\gamma\|_{\rm F}-\frac{\langle\gamma,\;\widehat{\gamma}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\gamma}\|_{\rm F}}\right|=\left|\frac{\|\gamma\|_{\rm F}\cdot\|\widehat{\gamma}\|_{\rm F}-\langle\gamma,\;\widehat{\gamma}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\gamma}\|_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta}\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma\|_{\rm F}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Delta}\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

In fact, by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Delta}-\Delta|

\displaystyle=\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{1/2}\right\|_{\rm F}-\left\|{\cal B}\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{1/2}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\left\|\left(\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right)\times_{m=1}^{M}\Sigma_{m}^{1/2}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}\prod_{m=1}^{M}\left\|\Sigma_{m}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}d_{k}R}}{\sqrt{n}}+w_{1}\max_{1\leq k\leq M}\sqrt{\frac{d_{k}R}{nd_{-k}}}=o(\Delta).

Since
\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}=o(\Delta)
, it follows that
\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/(2\widehat{\Delta})\rightarrow\Delta/2
.
Then, we have
|\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{1,n})|\asymp|\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{2,n})|\asymp\Delta e^{-\frac{(\Delta/2)^{2}}{2}}=\Delta e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\Big)^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{1,n})+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\Big)^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{2,n})

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}\cdot\Delta\cdot e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\asymp\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}.

Then (
61
) can be further bounded such that

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{*}-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\asymp\Big(\frac{\Delta}{2}-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)e^{-\frac{(\Delta/2)^{2}}{2}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\cdot\left|\frac{\Delta}{2}-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\right|+O\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}.

Eventually we obtain
{\cal R}^{*}-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\lesssim\Delta^{-1}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}(\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\vee\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}^{4})
in the event
\Omega_{1}
with probability at least
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})-n^{-c_{1}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}e^{-c_{1}d_{m}}
.

Next, focus on
{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}^{*}
. We apply Taylor’s expansion to
{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})
:

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\phi\Big(-\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)+\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2-\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\phi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+O\left(\Delta\cdot e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\right)\Big(\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle-\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2}{\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)^{2}\;\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left\{\overline{\phi}\Big(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)+\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2+\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\phi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+O\big(\Delta\cdot e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\big)\Big(\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle+\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2}{\widehat{\Delta}}\Big)^{2}\;\right\}

(62)

where the remaining term can be obtained similarly as (
61
) by using the fact that
|\phi^{\prime\prime}(t_{n})|=O(\Delta\cdot e^{-\Delta^{2}/8})
. Now we aim to bound the following term:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle-\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|\langle\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-{\cal M}_{2}+\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|\langle\overline{{\cal X}}^{(1)}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle\right|+\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|\langle\overline{{\cal X}}^{(2)}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle\right|.

Note that, in the event
\Omega_{1}
,
\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\|_{\rm F}\leq\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}+\|{\cal B}\|_{\rm F}\lesssim\Delta
. By Lemma
G.8
, in an event
\Omega_{2}
with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\overline{{\cal X}}^{(k)}-{\cal M}_{k},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}},\quad k=1,2.

It follows that, in the event
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle-\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}},

(63)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle+\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2}{\widehat{\Delta}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}}.

(64)

Substituting (
63
) and (
64
) into (
E.3
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\left|{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}^{*}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2-\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\phi^{\prime}(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle/2+\langle\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{\widehat{\Delta}}\phi^{\prime}(\frac{\langle{\cal D},\;\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}\rangle}{2\widehat{\Delta}})+O\left(\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}\right)\right)\right|

Since
{\cal D}/2-(\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{1})+{\cal D}/2+(\widehat{\cal M}-{\cal M}_{2})={\cal D}-({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})=0
, then it follows that

\displaystyle\left|{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}^{*}\right|\lesssim\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}\right).

Finally, combining the two pieces, we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}^{*}+{\cal R}^{*}-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left\|\widehat{\cal B}^{\rm cp}-{\cal B}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}+\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}\right),

in the event
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
with probability at least
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})-n^{-c}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
.

Now consider the two case. On the one hand, when
\Delta=O(1)
, by (
60
), with probability at least
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})-n^{-c}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq C\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}+C\frac{w_{1}^{2}R}{\Delta^{2}}\max_{1\leq m\leq M}\frac{d_{m}}{nd_{-m}}.

On the other hand, when
\Delta\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, by(
60
), with probability at least
1-n^{-c}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}e^{-cd_{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}\right)+C\Delta e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\frac{w_{1}^{2}R\max_{1\leq m\leq M}d_{m}^{2}}{\Delta^{2}nd}+C\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{w_{1}^{2}R\max_{1\leq m\leq M}d_{m}^{2}}{\Delta^{2}nd}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Delta^{3}e^{-\Delta^{2}/8}\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}+\frac{w_{1}^{2}R\max_{1\leq m\leq M}d_{m}^{2}}{\Delta^{2}nd}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\exp\left(-\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{3\log(\Delta)}{\Delta^{2}}\right)\Delta^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}+\frac{w_{1}^{2}R\max_{1\leq m\leq M}d_{m}^{2}}{\Delta^{2}nd}\right),

where
3\log(\Delta)/\Delta^{2}
is an
o(1)
term as
n\to\infty
.

As
\Delta^{2}\asymp w_{1}^{2}+\cdots+w_{R}^{2}\gg w_{1}^{2}(d_{\max}/d)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{w_{1}^{2}R\max_{1\leq m\leq M}d_{m}^{2}}{\Delta^{2}nd}\ll\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}d_{m}R}{n}.

That is, the second part in the excess misclassification rate, which comes from the estimation accuracy of the mode-
m
precision matrix is negligible.

E.4
Proof of Theorem
3.4

Note that the proof is not straightforward, partly because the excess risk
{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
does not satisfy the triangle inequality required by standard lower bound techniques. A crucial approach in this context is establishing a connection to an alternative risk function.
For a general classification rule
\Upsilon
, we define
L_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\Upsilon)=\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\Upsilon({\cal Z})\neq\Upsilon_{\theta}({\cal Z}))
, where
\Upsilon_{\theta}({\cal Z})
is the Fisher’s linear discriminant rule introduced in (
2
). Lemma
G.10

allows us to transform the excess risk
{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
into the risk function
L_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})
, as shown below:

{\cal R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})-{\cal R}_{\rm opt}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\geq\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}\Delta}{8}e^{\Delta^{2}/8}\cdot L_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp}).

(65)

We then apply Lemma
G.12
to derive the minimax lower bound for the risk function
L_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{\rm cp})
.

We carefully construct a finite collection of subsets of the parameter space
{\cal H}
that characterizes the hardness of the problem.
Any
M
-th order tensor
{\cal M}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{M}}
with CP rank
R
can be expressed as
{\cal M}={\cal F}\times_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{A}_{m}
. Here, the latent core tensor
{\cal F}=\diag(w_{1},...,w_{R})
is a diagonal tensor of dimensions
R\times\cdots\times R
, i.e.
{\cal F}_{i,...,i}
is non zero for all
i=1,..,R
, and all the other elements of
{\cal F}
are zero.
Denote the loading matrices
\boldsymbol{A}_{m}=(\boldsymbol{a}_{1m},...,\boldsymbol{a}_{Rm})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}\times R}
for each mode
m=1,\ldots,M
.

First, let
\boldsymbol{A}_{m}
be a fixed matrix where the
(i,i)
-th elements,
i=1,...,R
, are set to one and all other elements are zero. Denote

\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{A}_{M}\otimes\boldsymbol{A}_{M-1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\boldsymbol{A}_{1}
. According to basic tensor algebra, this setup implies that

\vect({\cal M})=\boldsymbol{A}\vect({\cal F})
and
\|{\cal M}\|_{\rm F}=\|\vect({\cal F})\|_{2}
. Let
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}
be the basis vector in the standard Euclidean space whose first entry is 1 and 0 elsewhere, and
\boldsymbol{I}_{d}=[\boldsymbol{I}_{d_{m}}]_{m=1}^{M}
.
Define the following parameter space

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{0}=

\displaystyle\big\{\theta=({\cal M}_{1},\;{\cal M}_{2},\;\boldsymbol{I}_{d}):\;{\cal M}_{1}={\cal F}\times_{m=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{A}_{m},\;{\cal M}_{2}=-{\cal M}_{1};\ \vect({\cal F})=\epsilon\boldsymbol{f}+\lambda\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{f}\in\{0,1\}^{R},\boldsymbol{f}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=0\big\},

where
\epsilon=c/\sqrt{n}
,
c=O(1)
and
\lambda
is chosen to ensure that
\theta\in{\cal H}
such that

\displaystyle\Delta=({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})=4\|\epsilon\boldsymbol{f}+\lambda\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=4\epsilon^{2}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{2}^{2}+4\lambda^{2}.

In addition to
{\cal H}_{0}
, we also define
\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell}
, for
\ell\neq m
, as fixed matrices where the
(i,i)
-th elements,
i=1,...,R
, are set to one and all other elements are zero. Let
{\cal F}
be a diagonal tensor such that the
(i,...,i)
-th elements,
i=1,...,R
, are set to one, and all other elements are zero. It implies that
\|{\cal M}\|_{\rm F}=\|\boldsymbol{A}_{m}\|_{\rm F}
. For
m=1,...,M
, define the following parameter spaces

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{m}=\big\{

\displaystyle\theta=({\cal M}_{1},\;{\cal M}_{2},\;\boldsymbol{I}_{d}):\;{\cal M}_{1}={\cal F}\times_{k=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{A}_{k},\;{\cal M}_{2}=-{\cal M}_{1};\ \vect(\boldsymbol{A}_{m})=\epsilon\boldsymbol{g}_{m}+\lambda_{m}\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{g}_{m}\in\{0,1\}^{d_{m}R},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=0\big\},

where
\epsilon=c/\sqrt{n}
,
c=O(1)
and
\lambda_{m}
is chosen to ensure that
\theta\in{\cal H}
such that

\displaystyle\Delta=({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}({\cal M}_{2}-{\cal M}_{1})=4\|\epsilon\boldsymbol{g}_{m}+\lambda_{m}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=4\epsilon^{2}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}+4\lambda_{m}^{2}.

It is clear that
\cap_{\ell=0}^{M}{\cal H}_{\ell}\subset{\cal H}
. We shall show below separately for the minimax risks over each parameter space
{\cal H}_{\ell}
.

First, consider
{\cal H}_{0}
. By Lemma
G.13
, we can construct a sequence of
R
-dimensional vectors
\boldsymbol{f}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{f}_{N}\in\{0,1\}^{R}
, such that
\boldsymbol{f}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=0
,
\rho_{H}(\boldsymbol{f}_{i},\boldsymbol{f}_{j})\geq R/8,\;\forall 0\leq i<j\leq N
, and
R\leq(8/\log 2)\log N
, where
\rho_{H}
denotes the Hamming distance.
To apply Lemma
G.12
, for
